## Current execution-profile handoff (AM-83–AM-86)

Two eligible production profiles exist: `local_4060_cu130` and
`confessor_pascal_cu126`. A new scientific run or campaign must freeze one
profile before its first measurement and may not silently switch hosts. The
selected host is the sole writer. The ordinary publication handoff is verify →
reconcile → durable commit → authenticated GitHub HTTPS push → fetch/parity.
For the owner-authorized Pascal G8_C successor only, AM-86 permits authenticated
per-unit evidence to accumulate continuously in the separate mutable runtime on
`confessor`; Git publication may occur after the unattended campaign or at an
owner-selected manual checkpoint, with loss before publication explicitly
accepted as an owner custody risk. Internal hashes/contracts and final
verification determine validity, and complete commit/push/parity remains
mandatory before table freeze or G8_D release. Git commit signing is optional
prospectively. Historical signing facts are unchanged.

**This file is the single source of truth for where the four-phase sequence stands.**
It is committed, so it survives a session dying mid-step. Prose in `NEXT.md` is a hand-off summary;
this file is the operational cursor. If they disagree, this file is right about progress and
`NEXT.md` needs updating.

Read this before anything else. Update it in the same commit as the work it describes.

## Rules

1. **Commit at every checkpoint.** Never let more than one checkpoint's worth of work sit
   uncommitted. Sessions end abruptly and without warning. **AM-86 exception:** the
   owner-authorized Pascal successor's separate runtime may accumulate authenticated per-unit
   evidence until its unattended invocation exits or the owner selects a manual checkpoint;
   unpublished loss is accepted, but final publication/parity remains mandatory.
2. **Mark a step `in-progress` and commit that *before* starting it** if the step is long or
   expensive (a campaign, a sweep, a multi-file refactor). A crash then leaves evidence of where
   you were, not silence.
3. **Work-in-progress commits go on `main` with a `wip(<phase>):` prefix.** They are expected to be
   non-green. Push every one — an unpushed commit is not durable. Do not rewrite pushed history.
4. **A phase ends with one green commit** using its real conventional-commit message, after which
   every step below is `done` and the full verification block passes.
5. **Never `git reset`, `git checkout --`, `git clean`, or stash to "tidy up" on cold start.**
   Uncommitted changes are the previous session's unfinished work. Inspect and finish or commit
   them; do not discard them.
6. **Record observed facts here** (test counts, selected classes, SHAs) so the next session does not
   pay to re-derive them. Mark anything not yet re-verified.

## Cold-start protocol

    git status --short
    git log -12 --oneline
    git rev-parse HEAD && git fetch origin && git rev-parse origin/main

Then:

* If HEAD ≠ origin/main, or the worktree is dirty, reconcile that first. Uncommitted work belongs
  to the step marked `in-progress` below — read the diff before touching it.
* If the top commit is a `wip(...)` commit, the phase is mid-flight. Resume at the first step below
  that is not `done`.
* If a step is marked `in-progress`, do **not** assume its outputs are correct. Re-verify that one
  step's outputs from scratch, then continue.
* Only steps marked `done` may be trusted without re-checking.

| C1.1 independently verify interleaver ownership | done | **audit CONFIRMED against installed source, not prose.** Sionna 2.0.1. Encoder stores `num_bits_per_symbol` at `encoding.py:172`, builds `_out_int`/`_out_int_inv` at `:173-179` from `generate_out_int` (`:303-339`, formula `perm_seq[i + j*Qm] = i*(n/Qm) + j`), and applies `c_out = c_out[..., self._out_int]` at `:791-793` — **after** the rate-matching puncture/selection at `:770-780`. Decoder reaches through the encoder instance: `llr_ch_reshaped[:, self._encoder.out_int_inv]` at `decoding.py:1646-1649` (multi-RV branch `:1636-1637`), **before** rate recovery at `:1654-1667`; `:1693-1694` re-applies `out_int` on the non-infobit path. Pairing is automatic and cannot be disabled while the encoder carries a Qm. **Probe (`k=200,n=400,bg2`): `encoder.out_int` is bit-identical to the project's `interleaver_indices(n,Qm)` for Qm ∈ {1,2,4}, and `out_int_inv == argsort(...)`; Qm=1 is the identity permutation, Qm∈{2,4} are not. Second probe: `encode(num_bits_per_symbol=Qm)` == `interleave(encode(no Qm), Qm)` exactly.** So `channel_transport.modulate()`'s `interleave()` is a *second application of the same permutation*; `demodulate()`'s `deinterleave()` is undone again by the decoder. Transmitted order = permutation squared ≠ identity for QPSK/16-QAM. BPSK unaffected. Round-trip tests missed it because the paired errors cancel and bit counts are permutation-invariant. Technical note appended to `worklogs/w4-classical-baseline-progress.md`. |
| C1.2 independent conformance and mutation tests | done | New module `tests/test_classical_interleaver_conformance.py` (26 tests). **Pre-repair result, recorded deliberately: `12 failed, 14 passed in 5.21s`** — the expected duplicate-permutation failure. The reference is independent of the code under test: a test-only `_ts_38212_out_int(E, Qm)` spelled as `np.arange(E).reshape(Qm, E//Qm).T.reshape(-1)` (not the project's index generator), composed with `_encode_uninterleaved()` — a raw `LDPC5GEncoder` built *without* `num_bits_per_symbol`, so it rate-matches and stops. Two guard tests prove the reference itself is right (`out_int` / `out_int_inv` agreement for Qm ∈ {1,2,4}; identity only at Qm=1) and **both pass pre-repair**, so the 12 failures cannot be blamed on a wrong reference. Also added a seam to `src/baseline/classical/channel_transport.py`: `mapper_input_bits()` (the exact bit sequence handed to the mapper, now the body of `modulate()`) and `split_llr_blocks()` (exact `E_r` cutting, now used by `demodulate()`) — a pure refactor at this step, no behaviour change. **Failing node IDs:** `test_mapper_input_is_the_rate_matched_once_interleaved_sequence[qpsk]` and `[qam16]`; `test_mapper_input_is_not_the_doubly_interleaved_sequence[qpsk]` and `[qam16]`; `test_mapped_symbols_are_the_reference_symbols`; `test_encoded_blocks_reach_the_mapper_unchanged[qpsk]` and `[qam16]`; `test_multi_code_block_concatenation_is_in_plan_order`; `test_demapper_llrs_reach_the_decoder_unchanged_except_for_splitting[qpsk]` and `[qam16]`; `test_mutation_additional_receiver_inverse_interleaver`; `test_mutation_incorrect_code_block_concatenation`. **Exact assertions:** the primary one is `assert np.array_equal(observed, expected)` at line 143 — `observed` and `expected` have *identical shape* (3072 for QPSK, 6144 for 16-QAM, 12800 multi-block) and differ only in order, which is precisely a permutation defect and not a length/count defect. The last two failures are the *clean-baseline* halves of mutation tests (`assert np.array_equal(clean[0], flat[:e_r])`, `assert np.array_equal(mapper_input_bits(blocks), expected)`) — they fail because the unmutated code is already wrong, which is itself the defect evidence. **`[bpsk]` passes**, confirming the scope conclusion that Qm=1 is the identity permutation. |
| C1.3 repair classical interleaver ownership | done | **Repair, entirely inside `src/baseline/classical/channel_transport.py`.** `mapper_input_bits()` no longer applies `interleave()` per block — it concatenates the adapter's already-interleaved blocks and nothing else. `demodulate()` no longer applies `deinterleave()` — it max-log-demaps and returns `split_llr_blocks()` at exact `E_r` boundaries, so the paired Sionna decoder's `out_int_inv` is the only inverse. Dropped the now-unused `interleave`/`deinterleave` imports; rewrote the module docstring's interleaver clause to name Sionna as the single owner. `_require_interleaver()` and `_require_fixed_normalisation()` are still called — they are what make the adapter's Qm argument mandatory. **Preserved:** exact `k × Qm` accounting, partial final code blocks, fixed mapping/normalisation, no per-packet rescaling, realised energy + PAPR, shared AWGN via `_shared_channel()`, keyed noise identity, the four verdicts, exact codestream recovery. **Standalone utilities in `src/baseline/ldpc/modulation.py` untouched** — they are G-2 known-answer material and the conformance test's cross-check. **Four PB_1 tests updated, not deleted, because they asserted the defect:** `test_modulation_applies_only_the_fixed_constellation_normalisation` (its `expected` re-interleaved the blocks by hand); `test_qam16_bit_interleaver_is_required_and_actually_changes_the_symbols` and `test_qam16_interleaver_is_not_a_no_op` (both asserted `modulate() != map_bits(concat(blocks))` — true only *because* of the duplicate; both now assert the same claim at the seam that owns it, `adapter.encoder.num_bits_per_symbol == Qm` and `out_int != arange`, **plus** that the project adds nothing on top); `test_disabled_qam16_interleaver_is_rejected_and_corrupts_the_link` part (b) (patched the removed `channel_transport.interleave`; now injects a *second* application — the PB_1C defect itself — and asserts the link dies at 20 dB). **Targeted result: `79 passed in 10.74s`** across `test_classical_interleaver_conformance.py` + `test_classical_transport.py` + `test_classical_mutations.py` + `test_classical_pipeline.py`. All 12 previously failing conformance tests now pass. |
| C1.4 enforce configured explicit axes | done | **Bypass closed in `src/baseline/classical/pipeline.py::_encode_source`.** PB_1 only checked that an explicit `encode_axis_px` did not upscale, so an unconfigured axis (e.g. 28 px for CIFAR-10) reached OpenJPEG and produced cache keys and evidence for a configuration the spec never authorised. An explicit axis is now a *selection* from `configured_axes(dataset, canonical_shorter_side)` — membership is required, and both the upscale check and the membership check run **before** `codec_downsample` or `encode_to_budget`. No second configuration source: the ladder still comes only from `params.baseline.downsample_axis_px`. **Tests added to `tests/test_classical_pipeline.py`** (13 → 17): `test_configured_explicit_axis_is_accepted` (32 px succeeds, `axes_attempted == (32,)`); `test_unconfigured_explicit_axis_is_rejected` (28 px — smaller *and* non-upscaling — raises `is not configured`); `test_unconfigured_explicit_axis_is_rejected_before_the_codec_runs` (monkeypatches both `J2KCodec.encode_to_budget` and `pipeline.codec_downsample` to raise, then asserts the unconfigured *and* the upscaling axis are still rejected — proving rejection precedes codec execution); `test_automatic_axis_iteration_is_descending_and_only_configured_axes` (spies on `codec_downsample`, asserts the attempted sequence is descending, a subset of `configured_axes`, and starts at the largest). Retained `test_requested_axis_may_not_upscale_the_source` and `test_configured_axes_are_descending_and_never_upscale`. **17 passed.** |
| C1.5 adjudicate the JPEG-2000 resolution issue | done | **Verdict: `unresolved with explicit downstream block`.** Searched `spec/SPEC.md` for a normative rule that resolves `j2k_resolutions = 6` against CIFAR-10's `[32, 24, 16]`: **there is none.** BR-1/AM-51 freeze `j2k_resolutions` as a flat scalar; AM-58 makes `downsample_axis_px` dataset-specific with `downsample_axis_never_upscales` as its only invariant. The two were frozen in different amendments and never checked against each other — the transparency probe ran Imagenette only (160/128/96/64, all ≥ 32 px). PB_1 correctness does **not** require changing it (CIFAR-10 is a DEC-1 smoke path, its 32 px axis works, and the pipeline already reports the other two honestly), so no frozen parameter was touched and **no AM entry was added**. Candidates recorded, neither selected: (1) drop 24/16 from `downsample_axis_px.cifar10`; (2) clamp `j2k_resolutions` to `min(6, floor(log2(axis))+1)`. Either needs an AM — both change the codec configuration hash and therefore **every** J2K cache key, for every dataset. **Blocks PB_3, the full BR-4 sweep and G-8; does not block PB_2.** Executable reproduction added: `tests/test_classical_pipeline.py::test_j2k_resolutions_cannot_encode_cifar10s_small_axes` asserts 32 px → `budget_exceeded` while 24/16 px → `codec_configuration_error`, and that the split is not a budget artefact (32 px succeeds at a generous budget). Recorded prominently in `RESUME.md` (facts table), `NEXT.md` (new "Open block" section) and the W4 worklog. `tests/test_classical_pipeline.py` 17 → 18, all pass. |

Expected: realised symbol energy and PAPR are measurements over the *realised symbol sequence*, and PB_1 mapped the §5.4.2.2 permutation squared, so it grouped bits into 16-QAM symbols differently. **BPSK and QPSK are unchanged** — BPSK because Qm=1 is the identity permutation, QPSK because its constellation is constant-modulus (Es ≡ 1.0, PAPR ≡ 0 dB) regardless of bit order. `noise_id`/`unit_noise_sha256` differ from B1.6 for an unrelated reason: a different `ChannelIdentity` fixture, not a transport change. **The B1.6 QPSK/16-QAM `Es`/`PAPR` figures in the PB_1 B1.6 row above are SUPERSEDED** — they describe a non-conformant transmit order. The residual `bytes_sent − header − payload` is zero payload filler, which has **no schema column** and is recorded in `accounting_examples.json` rather than folded into either column. Traced to `params.baseline.container_policy` ("all emitted container bytes count against payload_bits") plus BR-11's purpose ("so the fraction of the budget spent on format overhead is visible"). **This is an interpretation, not a quotation** — BR-11 does not spell the two columns out arithmetically, and a reader could instead take `payload_bytes` to mean `payload_bits/8`. never from chat context). Append-only, and it does **not** erase the PB_2 rows above: PB_2's
implementation happened, and PB_2C records that its *completion judgment and bounded evidence* are
being corrected.

`instructions/PB_2C.txt` §18 carries an **approved addendum** that is binding: two normative
decisions (the transparency-probe codec-configuration drift record; `analysis_version` 1 → 2) and
four clarifications (three separate raw noise fields; what preserving analysis-version-1 evidence
means; the bounded scope of `overhead_table.json`; and no empty commits when designating the
runner-ready and final green SHAs). Read §18 before C2.1.

| Psot byte offset | **6**, was 4. SOT segment layout is `SOT(2) | Lsot(2) | Isot(2) | Psot(4) | TPsot(1) | TNsot(1)`, so Psot begins six bytes into the segment. At offset 4 the parser read `Isot || high16(Psot)`; with `Isot = 0` and `Psot < 65536` that reads as **zero**, which silently took the `Psot = 0` last-tile fallback in `_next_tile_boundary` and landed on the correct boundary *by luck*. **The committed PB_2 overhead numbers are therefore not wrong** — but a multi-tile-part or large codestream would have been mis-split with no error. Added validation: `Lsot` must be exactly 10, Psot must not run past the codestream, and Psot must at least cover its own SOT segment | C2.4 | yes |
| known-answer codestream split | **Six fixtures, asserting the two counts individually rather than only that they sum.** (1) Single tile-part with a real non-zero `Psot`: `header = 34` (SOC 2 + SIZ 16 + SOT 12 + SOD 2 + EOC 2), `payload = 192`, total 226. (2) Multi-tile-part: `header = 32`, `payload = 82` (32 + 50). (3) `Psot = 0` last tile-part: `header = 18`, `payload = 36`. (4) A discrimination test asserting the offset-4 read differs from the true `Psot`, so the fixture genuinely exercises the offset. (5) Malformed cases — truncated, missing SOC, wrong `Lsot` — all rejected. (6) A **real OpenJPEG encode** reconciling exactly against `emitted_byte_count`. `header + payload == len(codestream)` is deliberately not relied on: a wrong boundary satisfies it | C2.4 | yes |
| decode-failure byte aggregation result | **Fixed.** `score_result` now returns the byte split whenever a codestream was emitted, so `decode_failure` rows carry `header_bytes`/`payload_bytes`; only `structural_infeasibility` and `codec_infeasibility`, where no codestream exists, stay null. The runner fills `header_bytes_values`/`payload_bytes_values` from **all** rows in the cell rather than from `delivered` only. An all-decode-failure cell therefore keeps `coverage_rate = 0`, null PSNR/SSIM, populated PAPR **and populated overhead** — the regime where format overhead dominates the budget, and precisely what PB_2 blanked. Field-semantics denominators and `BYTE_ACCOUNTING_NOTE` rewritten to AM-81's wording | C2.4 | yes |
| full-row timing result | **Fixed.** `run_row` starts its timer before the dataset fetch and stamps `wall_clock_s` on the last line of both return paths, so source coding, transport, classifier inference, reconstruction metrics, outage scoring and record construction are all inside it. PB_2 bracketed only `run_classical_pipeline`, excluding the classifier — the most expensive part of a delivered row. Proved with a deterministic fake clock rather than a stopwatch | C2.4 | yes |
| resume timing result | **Fixed.** The summary total is now `sum(row["wall_clock_s"] for row in rows.values())` over every durable row. PB_2 used `perf_counter()` measured from *after* the resume load, so a resumed run reported a total smaller than the sum of its own aggregate rows (which were already computed correctly as per-cell sums) | C2.4 | yes |
| OpenJPEG preflight result | **Fixed.** `assert_j2k_runtime()` and `loaded_openjpeg_version(required=True)` now run in `main()` **before** `evidence_dir.mkdir`, before the outage policy loads, before the classifier loads and before any cache entry. Previously the first check fired inside `J2KCodec.encode_to_budget` on the first encode, so a wrong OpenJPEG left a half-built evidence directory behind — contradicting the docstring on `src/env.py:119`. Uses the existing shared implementation; a test asserts the runner's symbols *are* `env`'s, so no second version parser can appear. A forced-failure test asserts no results directory is created | C2.4 | yes |
| actual OpenJPEG version | **2.5.4**, matching `params.environment.openjpeg`. Recorded in the execution metadata as `openjpeg_version`, alongside `openjpeg_preflight_preceded_artifacts` | C2.4/C2.7 | yes |

The verifier requires the final file and re-hashes it before deriving anything from it | C2.5 | yes |

| B3.1 `composition.py` arithmetic | done | **Files:** `src/baseline/classical/composition.py` (new), `tests/test_classical_composition.py` (new). `transport_block_success_probability()` is the product over code blocks of `1 - BLER_r`; `expected_accuracy()` is AM-51's mixture, keyword-only and **typed so a bare float cannot be passed for either accuracy term**; `compose()` returns a `CompositionResult` whose `as_record()` carries both measured inputs forward. `MeasuredCodecAccuracy` and `MeasuredOutageAccuracy` take **counts plus provenance**, never a ratio, so an assumed value has nowhere to enter; `MeasuredCodecAccuracy` additionally refuses any split but `val` (SR-22). `measured_outage_accuracy_from_record()` reads PB_2's committed `outage_policy.json` by its **counts**, cross-checks the recorded float against them, and refuses a record under another `baseline.outage_policy`. An empty code-block sequence raises rather than returning a vacuous `P = 1.0`. **Tests: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_classical_composition.py -q` → 31 passed.** Covers 1/2/many blocks, mixed BLERs, `P` exactly 0 and exactly 1, invalid probabilities (negative, >1, NaN, str, None, bool), the empty-product defect, both worked examples hand-checked off-implementation, the measured-not-reconstructed property using a deliberately non-stratified 137/1000 outage measurement, and the wrong-outage-score mutation. `check_literals` 0 findings (43 files). Next action: implement B3.2. |
| B3.2 BLER lookup + support guard | done | **Files:** `src/baseline/classical/composition.py`, `tests/test_classical_composition.py`. `BlerTable` is built by `g2_bler_table()` from the committed `results/baseline/g2/bler_results.csv`, **hash-bound** to the SHA-256 that `g2_adjudication.json` records for that file, so a tampered curve fails closed before it can feed a selection. Only the `sionna` arm is used; the `reference` rows are G-2's independent cross-check, not a second usable curve. `BlerIdentity.from_mapping()` requires the **complete** key — all eight fields of `params.baseline.ldpc_bler_reference_must_match` **plus `rate`**, which the committed evidence fixes and the spec's list omits (strictly narrower than required, the safe direction) — and rejects both missing and unrecognised fields. Support: exactly **six** identities (bpsk/qpsk/qam16 × the two declared SNR conventions `eb_n0_per_information_bit` and `es_n0_per_symbol`), each `K=128, N=256, BG2, Z=22, rate 0.5, offset_min_sum, offset 0.5, 50 iterations, 5000 trials/point`. Eb/N0 spans: bpsk/qpsk `[1.5, 2.75]`, qam16 `[4.0, 5.25]`. A mismatch returns `UNCHARACTERIZED` with reason `identity_not_characterized`; an SNR outside the span returns `snr_outside_characterized_support`. `.require()` raises `UncharacterizedBlerError`; `bler` is `None`, **never 0.0**. Interpolation happens only strictly inside the span and only in the representation `bler_reference.json` declares (`linear_in_snr_vs_log10_bler`); the module raises `NotImplementedError` if that declaration changes, and refuses to interpolate through a non-positive measured BLER. **Tests: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_classical_composition.py -q` → 69 passed** (31 from B3.1 + 38 here). Mutation coverage: each identity component altered individually (`k_and_n` ×2, `base_graph`, `lifting_size`, `modulation` ×2, `decoder_algorithm`, `decoder_offset`, `iterations`, `snr_convention`, `rate`), each required field deleted individually, an extra field added, a malformed `k_and_n`, six out-of-support SNRs including 18 dB (where a silent extrapolation would read as BLER 0), a wrong modulation not reusing the same K/N, a convention crossed between columns, and a tampered evidence file. Interpolation is checked against `sqrt(a*b)` at the midpoint — the geometric mean, not the implementation restated. `check_literals` 0 findings. Next action: implement B3.3. |
| B3.3 candidate cache + tie-break | done | **Files:** `src/baseline/classical/composition.py`, `tests/test_classical_composition.py`. `Candidate(dataset, ratio, modulation, ldpc_rate, encode_axis_px, snr_db)` validates **in `__post_init__`** that every declared field is classified either into `FEASIBILITY_KEY_FIELDS` or into `FEASIBILITY_KEY_EXCLUSIONS` *with a written reason* — a future field added to the candidate cannot silently fall out of the cache key. The sole exclusion is `snr_db`, because structural feasibility reads the transport-block geometry and codec payload budget and neither is a function of SNR; a test asserts the exclusion behaves that way rather than trusting the comment. `FeasibilityCache` counts hits/misses and refuses a probe that does not return a `Feasibility`. `select_best()` ranks only `ELIGIBLE` candidates by `TIE_BREAK_ORDER` — expected accuracy desc, `P(TB success)` desc, `Qm` asc, LDPC rate asc, encode axis desc, canonical `candidate_id` asc — where the last key makes the order **total**, which is what makes the result independent of enumeration order. Tie detection uses **exact** float equality; a tolerance would be an unpreregistered free parameter. `UNCHARACTERIZED` and `INFEASIBLE` candidates are carried into the record for audit but are **never ranked**: an uncharacterized cell is ineligible, not low-scoring, and has no score to rank with. With no eligible candidate the selection returns `selected=None, reason='no_eligible_candidate'` rather than guessing. **Tests: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_classical_composition.py -q` → 91 passed** (69 + 22 here): cache-key completeness field by field, the SNR exclusion, cached/uncached equivalence, cross-configuration collision over all five keyed fields, probe-contract refusals, tie-breaking at each documented level, totality, order-independence over all 24 permutations of a four-candidate set, uncharacterized-is-ineligible, infeasible-is-ineligible, and the empty-eligible case. `check_literals` 0 findings. Next action: implement B3.4. |
| B3.4 system modes + two-pass limit | done | **Files:** `src/baseline/classical/composition.py`, `tests/test_classical_composition.py`. **Modes:** `CLASSICAL_ADAPTIVE` / `CLASSICAL_FIXED_MOD` / `CLASSICAL_FIXED_MCS` map to three distinct `SystemModePolicy` signatures over *what may move between SNR points* — `(adapts_modulation, adapts_ldpc_rate, adapts_encode_axis)` = `(T,T,T)` / `(F,T,T)` / `(F,F,F)`. `mode_policy()` re-checks `params.artifacts.system_values` and `params.baseline.modulation_tuning == adaptive_per_snr` at call time and raises `NotImplementedError` if either moves. `resolve_curve()` makes the modes behaviourally distinct: adaptive re-selects per SNR; fixed-mod picks the one modulation whose per-SNR bests sum highest and adapts underneath it; fixed-MCS selects once at `baseline.fixed_mcs_design_snr_db` (**7 dB**) and holds the whole configuration, **refusing to snap** to a neighbouring grid point if the design SNR is absent, and recording `fixed_mcs_packet_count` = 1. **Two-pass limit is structural:** `SelectionCampaign` reads the permitted passes from `reference_classifier.br4_selection_passes` / `br4_selection_terminates_after_pass` (both **2**, cross-checked against each other) and refuses a repeat pass, an out-of-order pass, a non-integer or unknown identifier, a third pass, a pass reusing an earlier pass's scorer, and a resumed state that repeats a pass, carries an unknown pass, or ran under another mode. `PassContext.result_of()` refuses any pass id **≥** the current one, so pass one cannot read pass two even through a context retained past its own pass; `PassResult` is a frozen dataclass, so pass two cannot edit pass one. **PB_3 trains nothing** — `reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_gate` is G-8 and the second-pass scorer is supplied by the caller. **Tests: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_classical_composition.py -q` → 125 passed** (91 + 34 here). Next action: implement B3.5. |
| B3.5 sweep budget guard | done | **Files:** `src/baseline/classical/composition.py`, `tests/test_classical_composition.py`. `select_operating_points()` is the ordinary BR-4 entry point and runs `check_sweep_budget()` **before any work**. Unauthorized limits: `MAX_UNAUTHORIZED_CANDIDATES=64`, `MAX_UNAUTHORIZED_SAMPLES=25`, `MAX_UNAUTHORIZED_WORKLOAD=512` — three separate checks, because a sweep can be too large in three ways, and each refusal names the limit that fired. Above any of them the call raises `SweepBudgetError` unless a typed `G8Authorization(gate='G-8', authorized_by, reason, max_candidates, max_samples)` is passed explicitly; an authorization permits only its own declared limits. Validation lives in `_validate_authorization()` and runs **both** at construction and at use, so an instance built through `object.__new__` (skipping `__post_init__`) is still refused. **Deliberate absences, each tested:** no environment variable is read anywhere in the module (asserted by scanning its own source for `os.environ`/`getenv`/`environ[`/`putenv`); `authorization` defaults to `None` on every entry point (asserted via `inspect.signature`); and **no tracked non-test file in the repository constructs a `G8Authorization`** (asserted by scanning `git ls-files`). **Tests: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_classical_composition.py -q` → 147 passed** (125 + 22 here): bounded fixture accepted, candidate/sample/combined workloads refused, exact boundary probes at each limit (limit accepted, limit+1 refused — the mutation that flips `>` to `>=` fails here), absent authorization refused, eight malformed-authorization variants, a duck-typed look-alike refused, a validation-bypassing forgery refused, non-count arguments refused, and the three repository-default absences. Three PB_3 choices are *stricter* than the spec, and being stricter is not a change to it: (a) requiring `rate` in the BLER identity — the spec's must-match list omits it while the committed evidence fixes it, so demanding it can only reject lookups the spec would also have to reject; (b) the documented tie-break order — the spec fixes the objective function and is silent on exact ties, so any deterministic total order satisfies it, and the alternative is an order that emerges from dictionary iteration; (c) the sweep budget — an implementation safety boundary with no scientific content, which constrains nothing the spec asserts. | **~~OPEN ISSUE~~ — RESOLVED by AM-80 at PB_2C/C2.1: `downsample_axis_px.cifar10 = [32]`. Original observation follows. `j2k_resolutions` vs CIFAR-10 axes** | `baseline.j2k_resolutions = 6` requires every tile dimension ≥ `2^5 = 32`, but `baseline.downsample_axis_px.cifar10 = [32, 24, 16]`. OpenJPEG hard-errors at 24 px and 16 px (*"Number of resolutions is too high in comparison to the size of tiles"*) for **every** image, so two of CIFAR-10's three configured axes cannot encode at all. Never caught before because the transparency probe ran Imagenette only (160/128/96/64). B1 records it per axis as `codec_configuration_error` rather than silently skipping. **Needs a spec decision (probably an AM): either clamp `j2k_resolutions` per axis to `min(6, log2(axis)+1)`, or drop 24/16 from the CIFAR-10 axis list.** Not decided in PB_1 — it changes a frozen codec parameter and therefore the codec configuration hash and every J2K cache key. | B1.2 | yes |

Values agree, names do not. **Deliberately not renamed** — the committed transparency-probe evidence records cache keys produced under the current spelling, so a rename would invalidate them. Tested by value. | B1.2 | yes |
| B1 J2K payload framing | no length is signalled; filler is zero bytes, so the receiver truncates at the **last** `ff d9` EOC in the padded payload. Verified byte-identical recovery on every delivered case. | B1.2 | yes |

## PB_3C — correct PB_3 selection semantics and interruption-safe resumed state

**Durable instruction: `instructions/PB_3C.txt`.** A narrow corrective phase between PB_3 and G-8.
PB_3 remains substantially accepted and is **not** reopened generally. This phase corrects four
specific things: fixed-modulation reference semantics, resumed `SelectionCampaign` validation,
pre-G-8 freezing of the selection tie-break order, and the G-8 handoff.

**Phase starting SHA: `32edbbb58983e54103b2f252c4d8d8f30aa2378e`** (the PB_3 green,
`feat(classical): add BR-4 selection infrastructure and W4 adjudication`). That is a historical
fact and does not move when the opening ledger commit lands.

**Scope guard — none of this happens in PB_3C:** no BR-4 sweep of any size, no real
`G8Authorization` in tracked non-test code, no operating ratio selected, no full-grid BLER evidence,
no training or fine-tuning, no DJSCC, no λ calibration, no ER-9, no test-split access, no
regeneration of PB_2C bounded measurements, no change to any existing scientific outcome, no change
to any file under `src/baseline/ldpc/`, and no broadening into a general audit.

an ordered array, so no dictionary ordering and no whitespace is identity, and JSON escaping makes delimiter injection non-colliding. **Output rule:** SHA-256, first 8 digest bytes as an unsigned big-endian integer, `0 <= seed < 2**64`, no modulo, zero valid. **Allowed purposes (closed):** `information_bits`, `awgn_real`, `awgn_imag`, each with its own stream. **RNG:** numpy `2.5.1`, `Philox`; `Philox.advance(n)` was measured to skip `4n` uint64 words, so the information-bit stream is an indexed raw-word stream with `bit_i = (word[i // 64] >> (i % 64)) & 1`, LSB-first, `uint8`, C order, trailing bits discarded and never carried; Gaussians are `Generator(Philox(key=seed)).standard_normal(count)`, `float64`, C order. **Chunk-boundary invariance:** measured, not assumed — `integers(0,2,dtype=uint8)` is **not** invariant and was therefore rejected as the bit API; the frozen bit stream is invariant at boundaries 0/1/62/63/64/65/127/128, at irregular chunkings and at lengths not divisible by 64, and is additionally randomly addressable; `standard_normal` is invariant under regular, irregular and single-element chunkings. **Resume granularity:** work-unit atomic — only a complete atomically committed result is resumable evidence, an interrupted unit is discarded and restarted from trial zero on the same seeds, there is no mid-work-unit trial cursor and no seek into a Gaussian stream, and shard layout or execution order never changes an output. **Fixed vectors (independently recomputed, not generated by the helpers under test):** material SHA-256 `54ce9ccd…a766e6`/`1c7c8fad…a50323`/`e4bb6658…65c3cb` and seeds `6110994150561148315`/`2052673504454730000`/`16481879790777643321` for information/real/imaginary; first Philox words `[3688305290296260437, 15804894354361011202, 15787647027830899325, 3711645746825559366]`; bits 0..7 `[1,0,1,0,1,0,1,0]`; bits 60..67 `[1,1,0,0,0,1,0,0]`; first `awgn_real` draws `[0.9216810155880256, 0.5781483793956664, 0.1512876155480005, -0.924367554903119]`; Wilson at 16 trials `[0.0, 0.19360768053443644]`, `[0.011119344764642547, 0.28328737570298934]`, `[0.8063923194655636, 1.0]`. **Schemas:** request schema version **1** with 20 closed fields (bindings, work unit, complete `BlerIdentity`, exact SNR, source packet IDs, `trials_requested`/`trial_count_source`, seed identity/domain/per-purpose records, `scientific_evidence`, always-false `merge_eligible`, `test_split_access=0`, visible label); result schema version **1** in four sections — identity, measurement, non-identity `execution_metadata`, disposition — with closed status enum `incomplete`/`complete`/`failed`. **Count-authoritative invariants:** `trials_completed <= trials_requested`, `block_errors <= trials_completed`, `information_bits = trials_completed x K`, `bit_errors <= information_bits`, negatives/booleans/NaN/infinity rejected, BER and BLER and both Wilson bounds recomputed from counts and counts override any stored float, zero completed trials report `null` rather than zero, completed evidence needs `trials_completed > 0`. **Zero-error and all-error behavior:** both are valid characterized evidence at the full trial count and both become merge eligible. **Full-strength/smoke separation:** smoke is capped at 3 work units and 16 trials per unit, carries `scientific_evidence=false`, `merge_eligible=false`, `required_coverage_contribution=0` and the visible label `NON-SCIENTIFIC BOUNDED SMOKE`, and can never satisfy the full-strength validator even when relabelled. **Confidence:** 95 percent Wilson score, project-owned; no general confidence parameter governs G-8 and `baseline.ldpc_bler_reference.confidence_percent` is G-2-specific and is not read; recorded as diagnostic only, not used in BR-4 ranking or eligibility and not a stopping rule; no adaptive stopping exists. **Contract ID:** `g8bler-878b218e60743dd5c85859348dfdbacdac847b344389d5688e182739e312dbbd`, derived from canonical contract content excluding the ID and independent of timestamps, absolute paths, hostname and commit SHA. **Contract artifact SHA-256:** `dfa1d68775b3720375dce7d71661935026d1b8674f020114df8568961fca7945` (11,924 bytes). **Bound sources:** only `src/baseline/g8_bler_contract.py`, `tools/gen_g8_bler_tooling_contract.py`, `tools/verify_g8_bler_tooling_contract.py`; no future runner, shard, checkpoint or merge file, and never the artifact's own hash. **State registration:** `tools/register_g8_artifact.py` appended exactly one binding for `results/baseline/g8/bler_tooling_contract.json` and set the B2 restart command; state SHA-256 `4b47e62aca0cd61930d9e389908b3dbdfb31e4f6d0be452fc9cd2cf0dfc2c3ab`, produced artifacts 3, both G8_A bindings byte-identical. **Unchanged campaign hashes:** campaign ID `g8-8acd86ad…900a`, manifest SHA-256 `0e9504ab…22d1`, required-identity SHA-256 `b8f7540a…2b77`, selection policy `6a4ffa98…6db6`. **Exact tests/results:** starting full suite after the marker **1228 passed, 0 failed**; follow_symlinks=False)` descriptor-relative, which cannot replace → directory `fsync` → safe staging removal → reread and validate. The final pathname is never opened for writing and there is no direct-write fallback; an unavailable no-replace primitive fails closed with `AtomicStateError`. **Replacement:** one exclusive per-unit critical section — `fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX)` on `.locks/<digest>.lock` plus a process-local keyed `threading.Lock` — holding the reread, digest comparison, terminal/idempotence check, transition validation, staged write, descriptor-relative `os.replace`, directory `fsync` and post-install reread. **Process races:** eight simultaneous creator *processes* → exactly one exit 0 and seven conflicts; two processes proposing different valid successors from the same predecessor digest → exactly one success and exactly one `StaleWriterError`, final bytes equal the winner's proposal, over 5 stress rounds; threads in one process likewise yield exactly one winner. **Hard exits:** exit before publication leaves the final path absent and a retry succeeds; exit after publication leaves complete canonical JSON, never partial; orphan staging artifacts are never read as state and never block a retry. **Descriptors:** an instrumented `os.close`/`os.open` tracker proves no descriptor integer is closed twice across forced failures in `os.open`, `os.fdopen`, `stream.write`, `stream.flush`, `os.fsync`, `os.link`, `os.replace`, directory fsync, staging unlink and post-install reread; every one raises the intended domain error, never a secondary `EBADF`, and the original exception is preserved as `__cause__`. **Paths:** every `exists() and is_symlink()` boundary was replaced with no-follow `os.lstat`, so dangling symlinks at the final name, the root, the bucket and the lock path are all rejected; the tautological two-`resolve()` comparison was removed; a parent-directory symlink swap between validation and open cannot redirect publication, which fails closed instead. **Durability:** `EACCES` is no longer treated as "unsupported" — any directory-fsync failure becomes `AtomicStateError` and no caller can report success. **Transitions:** `result_linked` requires a non-null lowercase-hex `request_sha256`, result path and SHA, `scientific_execution_performed == true`, `trials_completed > 0`, `test_split_access == 0`, and no result may exist without a request binding in any status; Logical repository-relative paths are rendered from `results/baseline/g8/work_units` and never carry the caller-supplied physical root. **Context:** wraps `AuthenticatedUnitStateContext` (itself wrapping `AuthenticatedExecutionContext`), reverifies all nine immutable authority fields plus the exact B2C contract ID/SHA, builds one immutable digest→work-unit-ID reverse index at construction, and authenticates the registered B3 contract only when campaign state binds it — `require_resume_contract=True` fails closed, and the generator bootstrap is the only caller allowed to proceed without it. An instrumented test patches `g8_bler_contract._required_work_unit_bytes` and proves 200 per-unit lookups trigger **0** reloads of the 8.6 MB artifact. **Lock:** `<root>/legacy root-local lock design (superseded)` with `fcntl.flock`, `LOCK_EX` for reconciliation and `LOCK_SH` for future workers; lock order is fixed as `("global_reconciliation_lock", "per_unit_b2c_lock")`; a symlink or dangling symlink at the lock path is rejected; there is no unlocked fallback. **An absent root is never created by taking the lock** — a worker must create the root while taking this same lock, so an absent root proves no worker is mid-flight, and this is what keeps read-only inspection from materialising the production tree. **Census:** no-follow `os.lstat`/`os.scandir` over a closed allowed set (two-lowercase-hex buckets, `.locks/`, `legacy root-local lock design (superseded)`, the three canonical filenames, and B2C staging reported as ignored orphan staging); it rejects symlinks and dangling symlinks at every level, unknown top-level entries, non-directory buckets, wrong-bucket placement, uppercase or unknown digests, malformed attempt tokens, alternate extensions, unrecognized temporaries, nested bucket directories, non-regular authoritative objects and hard-linked aliases (`st_nlink != 1`). Output is ordered solely by the frozen required-work-unit order and explicit numeric attempt: a test shuffles `os.scandir` output with a seeded RNG over five rounds and gets byte-identical JSON, and attempts sort `[1, 2, 3, 10, 21]` rather than lexically. **Process evidence:** real `os.fork` children prove exclusive excludes exclusive, shared coexists with shared, exclusive excludes shared, and `kill -9` on a holder releases the lock so a later acquisition does not hang. **Tests:** `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_g8_bler_resume.py -q` → **85 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 warnings**; `tests/test_g8_bler_work_units.py tests/test_g8_bler_contract.py` → **377 passed** (unchanged); `tools/check_literals.py` → 47 files, 0 findings, 66 annotations; `git diff --check` clean. `repair_work_unit(...)` implementing exactly the two recovery-matrix rows; the closed `REPAIR_MODES` pair (`read_only` default, `repair_recoverable` opt-in); and `_repaired_state`, which builds the successor for each row directly from a clean claim and therefore never constructs the B2C-forbidden intermediate request-bound claim. **Locking correction found and fixed during implementation:** the first cut took `work_units.unit_state_lock` around the repair and then called `work_units.replace_unit_state`, which takes **the same** per-unit lock internally — the process-local keyed `threading.Lock` is not reentrant, so every repair self-deadlocked (observed as a hung pytest run, isolated to the two repair tests). B2C's `replace_unit_state` already rereads under its own lock and compares against the expected predecessor digest inside that same critical section, so the compare-and-swap **is** the linearization point; the outer acquisition bought nothing and could only deadlock. The repair now rereads state, request and result immediately before proposing, hands B2C the observed state SHA as `expected_previous_sha256`, and lets `StaleWriterError` reject any interleaving. **Recovery semantics:** a non-`StaleWriterError` publication failure never infers whether the atomic replacement landed — it rereads the installed authoritative bytes and only then decides; after a successful swap the unit is reclassified and required to land in `POST_REPAIR_CLASSIFICATIONS`, so `recoverable_failed_result -> failed_retryable` and `recoverable_complete_result -> completed_full_strength` are enforced rather than assumed. **Tests:** 14 new tests — read-only inspection changes no byte; the failed row repairs to a valid `failed` state carrying the request SHA, no result reference, `scientific_execution_performed = true`, the exact validated `trials_completed` and the same attempt and shard; the complete row repairs to a valid `result_linked` state with the exact derived logical result path, result SHA and 5,000 trials; repair never produces a request-bound claim; repair is idempotent (second call reports `repaired = false` and leaves the bytes identical); all four non-repairable shapes (`absent`, `claimed_unbound`, `claimed_request_published`, `failed_retryable`) are byte-for-byte untouched under `--repair-recoverable`; a mixed root repairs exactly the two recoverable units and leaves a third alone; a stale predecessor loses the compare-and-swap with `StateConflictError`; an unknown repair mode is refused; a real `os.fork` child holding the exclusive reconciliation lock makes an inspection **wait** rather than reconcile alongside it; and inspection never creates the production runtime root. A census-rejection was also confirmed incidentally: a sentinel file written inside the runtime root was rejected as an unknown top-level entry, so the fixture writes it outside. `tests/test_g8_bler_resume.py` → **135 passed** (was 121; +14) under both file order and randomized order; full suite → **1,797 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** (was 1,783; +14); `tools/check_literals.py` → 47 files, 0 findings, 66 annotations; B2C verifier with `--require-no-live-state` and phase-state both PASS; `git diff --check` clean; no production runtime tree, no runner, no simulation, no artifact and no campaign-state change. **Ending SHA:** this `feat(g8-b): add B3 explicit recovery under the reconciliation lock` commit, resolved from Git history after commit. **Exact next action:** B3.4 — implement deterministic resume plans and merge reports. |
